ADM-IE

BATUHAN YILDIRIM, Hale Demirtepe — 2026-09-13 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.22736303

This deposit contains the complete code and data for ADM-IE, a model of reductive debromination of polybrominated diphenyl ethers (PBDEs) in anaerobic sediment microcosms. ADM-IE couples a compartmental kinetic model, which fits pathway-specific rate constants to measured congener profiles, with a feed-forward neural network, which estimates rate constants for the debromination steps that cannot be resolved from the measurements. The data come from four sediment microcosms — a negative control, natural attenuation, biostimulation and bioaugmentation — in which 23 PBDE congeners were quantified at eight sampling times over 180 days. The workflow The reaction network is enumerated as every single-bromine removal connecting the quantified congeners, giving 129 candidate pathways. For 33 of them both the parent and the daughter congener are quantifiable, so a rate constant can be estimated directly; these are the marker pathways. For the remaining 96, at least one terminal congener stays below the quantification limit throughout the incubation, so they carry no kinetic information of their own. The coupled system dC/dt = A(k)C is integrated with ode45 and fitted separately to each microcosm by constrained least squares with multiple starting points, yielding one rate constant per marker pathway per microcosm, together with identifiability diagnostics. A neural network with one hidden layer is then trained on the marker rate constants of the three treated microcosms, using the Gibbs free energy of each debromination step, the bromination degree of the parent congener, the position of the removed bromine and the treatment as inputs, and the logarithm of the rate constant as the response. The network is validated by pathway-grouped nested cross-validation, in which every observation of a pathway is held out together, so the reported performance is the performance expected for a pathway that has never been observed. The stored network is finally applied to the 96 non-marker pathways, with an applicability-domain check against the training distribution. Contents matlab/ — the ODE model, the network training and validation code, the non-marker prediction code, and a single entry point, run_admie_pipeline.m, that runs the four microcosms in sequence and consolidates the output into one workbook.

OTSOW: Testing Finite-Time Traversable Wormhole Models in General Relativity: A Research Programme

Adam Obidowski — 2026-09-13 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.22735317

Description An Empirical Research Programme for Finite-Time Traversable Wormholes: From Astrophysical Screening to Conditional Transmission Tests This work develops a falsifiable research framework for investigating finite-time traversable wormholes in four-dimensional general relativity, connecting theoretical consistency with observational searches and, conditionally, future controlled transmission experiments. Rather than treating mathematical existence as evidence of physical realization, the programme establishes a hierarchy of evidential claims ranging from calibrated anomalies and model-dependent geometric identification to direct tests of information transmission through an identified traversable connection. The framework uses a finite-time wormhole model supported by a classical phantom scalar field and positive-energy radial null streams as a theoretical benchmark, while explicitly recognizing that the physical existence of the phantom source has not been established. The proposed programme combines source-consistent relativistic modelling with gravitational lensing, joint photometric–astrometric inference, strong-field and gravitational-wave tests, realistic competing astrophysical hypotheses, detector response modelling, and end-to-end injection–recovery procedures. Particular emphasis is placed on separating anomaly detection from wormhole identification and on calibrating sensitivity, false-positive rates, and selection effects using realistic observing windows and instrumental systematics. A central result is that the duration of traversability need not coincide with the timescale of an astronomical observable. For a static Ellis-lens benchmark with throat radius A=106A=10^6 m, the calculated Einstein crossing time is approximately 2.11 days, whereas an independently assumed opening interval of 10A/c10A/c lasts only about 0.0334 s. This difference of more than six orders of magnitude demonstrates that a short-lived traversability window cannot simply be inserted into a static lensing model without an explicit time-dependent propagation calculation. The study also presents an auditable finite-source lensing benchmark in which synthetic Ellis-wormhole light curves are fitted with an ordinary positive point-mass lens. The attained residual RMS differences range from approximately 0.31% to 1.44%, depending on source size. These values are treated strictly as deterministic model-comparison benchmarks rather than detection significances or evidence for observational identifiability, since realistic astrophysical alternatives, correlated systematics, and full survey selection effects have not yet been incorporated. At its strongest level, the programme specifies a conditional controlled transmission experiment requiring two independently localized and accessible wormhole mouths, a mapped exterior geometry, and a traversability interval long enough for signal exchange. The proposed protocol uses independent transmitters, receivers, clocks, and fresh challenge–response signals to test whether information follows a connection that cannot be explained by the mapped exterior causal routes. This stage is explicitly conditional: neither the benchmark construction nor present technology establishes the existence of such an experimentally accessible configuration. Overall, the article reframes traversable-wormhole research as a sequence of testable scientific questions. It provides a methodology for moving from mathematical models to observable predictions, calibrated astronomical searches, population constraints, and—if suitable candidates are ever identified—direct transmission experiments. The resulting programme is designed to produce scientifically meaningful outcomes whether the searches yield candidates, exclusions, or null results.

Passive Observer Fibers in Two-Level Quantum Dynamics: Local Rigidity, Global Ambiguity, and Controlled Active Completion

Tao Lin — 2026-09-13 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.22734739

We study identifiability of finite-dimensional Hermitian dynamics from complete site-to-site transition probabilities. On the two-level spectral stratum $H = \alpha I + \beta P$, passive probabilities factor through a simple projector-modulus observer. We give the exact static/visible classification of this observer and then exhibit two sharply different forms of nonidentifiability. For a rank-three harmonic projector on seven sites, one passive law has at least four pairwise diagonal-gauge-inequivalent preimages, while an exact cyclotomic tangent calculation proves local modulus rigidity at each of the four constructed branches. In contrast, a standard one-parameter qutrit SIC family gives a continuous family of gauge-inequivalent zero-diagonal Hamiltonians with identical complete passive probabilities. We formulate the fixed-diagonal tangent map and an equivalent Bargmann-weighted triangle-curl system, making the local/global contrast explicit. Finally, under a calibrated isolated edge-phase intervention model, two cubic-response settings recover a complex rooted triangle invariant and at most $2(n-2)$ settings reconstruct the full diagonal-gauge class on complete support. The results separate passive nonidentifiability, local differential rigidity, and active identifiability without requiring a generic rigidity theorem for projector-modulus fibers.

Representation-Covariant Scale Exponents under Fisher-Induced Whitening: Identifiability, Profile Likelihood, and Adaptive Estimation

Karimov Hikmat, Alekberli Rahid — 2026-09-13 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.22737564

The KA (Kerimov–Alekberli) model posits that Fisher Information spectral projectionsobey a Generalized Gaussian Distribution (GGD) whose scale and shape parametersfollow power laws of the corresponding eigenvalue, with exponents ν and α. Wecharacterise which exponents are representation-covariant artefacts under Fisher-induced whitening and which are intrinsic.Proposition 1 establishes an exact, finite-sample, distribution-free identity: theOLS estimate of ν shifts by exactly 1/2 under whitening, for any data and any shapeparameter.Theorem 1 extends this to the profile likelihood: the profile log-likelihood isequivariant under whitening, so the profile MLE satisfies ˆνPw = ˆνPraw + 1/2 andˆαPw = ˆαPraw exactly. Thus α is representation-invariant; ν is representation-covariantwith a universal +1/2 shift.Proposition 2 establishes the sign-identifiability boundary: perfect sign recoveryof ν does not imply unbiased estimation. An adaptive AIC selector between theshape-free OLS model (M0) and the profile GGD model (M1) achieves Type-I rate11% at α = 0 and power 1.00 for α ≥ 0.20.A Monte Carlo benchmark over 360 cells (36,000 runs) confirms all results atmachine precision: Gate 1 maximum deviation ≤ 5.6 × 10−9; profile RMSE 4–8×smaller than OLS. The BIS demonstration validates pipeline executability only; theLLM-0 protocol is a prospective specification for future empirical work.

Reproducibility archive: Hallucination you can predict -- physical hypothesis reasoning at the identifiability bound on evidence fusion

Topological Genome Engineering: Number-Theoretic Spectral Rigidity and Semiclassical Wave Scars as Active Structural Stabilizers in Synthetic Macromolecules

Mark Dreher — 2026-09-13 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.22698299

This monograph introduces a novel architectural paradigm at the intersection of non-equilibrium quantum dynamics, analytic number theory, and synthetic genomics to resolve the cellular metabolic energy-budget paradox. While traditional biophysics models treat non-coding genomic sequences as stochastic, ergodic polymers subjected to random thermal fluctuations, this framework establishes that macroscale macromolecular architectures can be engineered to bypass standard thermalization pathways. By utilizing a deterministic Base-4 transcoding matrix (), we project continuous, self-adjoint quantum operators acting on continuous Hilbert space directly onto discrete, quaternary genomic sequences. By spacing structurally essential coordinates to reflect the short-orbit periodic actions derived from the non-trivial zeros of the Riemann zeta function, the three-dimensional chromatin fold behaves as a structured quantum many-body scar (QMBS) manifold. This spatial layout enforces constructive and destructive phase interference across the conformational degrees of freedom of the polymer backbone, establishing a localized sub-terahertz phononic bandgap that prevents ambient environmental noise from thermalizing ergodically. To satisfy the constraints of non-equilibrium statistical mechanics, the resulting macromolecule functions as a self-sustaining non-ergodic phase-crystal that breaks translational symmetry across a multi-dimensional phase-space manifold, modulating the filtration cascade of high-dimensional state vectors as they distill down into localized spacetime thermalization. This theoretical framework is backed by a concrete, three-stage physical validation protocol: 1. Static Scale Profile (Cryo-smFRET Phase Cancellation): Mechanical distance variance is evaluated using dual-terminus labeled (5' Cy3 donor / 3' Cy5 acceptor) custom-synthesized 120 base-pair double-stranded DNA segments. The protocol compares a random control polynucleotide string (Construct A) against an experimental string (Construct B) engineered via matrix tracking to pattern rigid, triple-hydrogen-bonded G-C blocks strictly over coordinates mapping to finite prime steps up to 113. Following flash-vitrification into an amorphous ice lattice at 100 K, samples are subjected to a continuous broadband acoustic white-noise spectrum (10 Hz to 100 kHz at 50 mW) via a surface acoustic wave (SAW) piezoelectric transducer chip. Measurable success is defined by a rigorous statistical gate requiring Construct B to demonstrate a minimum 35% reduction in distance variance () compared to Construct A (). 2. Dynamic Scaling Matrix (Floquet Waveguide Optimization): Isothermal scaling rules are tested using extended polynucleotide lattices formatted as structured 2D DNA Origami rectangular tile arrays measuring 100 nm × 70 nm, built from a single-stranded M13mp18 viral DNA core scaffold (7,249 nucleotides) and a pool of 200 custom short synthetic staple oligonucleotides. Target staples mapping to log-weighted Riemann zero coordinates are modified with internal Biotin-TEG extensions and anchored to a streptavidin-coated mica substrate. Using an integrated thermal-step vitrified microscope enclosure, the sample experiences a continuous edge-driving force while the temperature is shifted dynamically from a 200.0 K cryo-baseline up to a 310.0 K physiological baseline. This driving force triggers a dynamic modulation across successive intervals of a boundless number-theoretic library of finite prime number sub-sequences (scaling from primes [2...113] up through extended sets [1009...7919]) to damp edge-mode kinetic wave packets. Success is governed by an automated conditional loop requiring the empirical model's predictive accuracy to exceed a strict confidence value of 0.950 across all sequential temperature tiers while maintaining a core transmission attenuation profile exceeding dB. 3. Inverse Semiclassical Barcoding (Universal Identification): Structural uniqueness and state-space path invariance are mapped using multi-atom networks and synthetic covalent/hydrogen-bonded macromolecular chains constructed via Incremental Defect Gating arrays on atom-flat mica pre-treated with 10 mM . The test method applies Incremental Defect Gating—the systematic, covalent addition of specific elemental groups one by one—to track the resulting resonance frequency and physical eigenvalue shifts. Surface structures are captured via non-contact tapping mode Cryogenic Atomic Force Microscopy (Cryo-AFM) at 100 K using a silicon nitride cantilever probe driven by integrated sub-nanometer resonant actuators operating at a node frequency of 12.5 kHz, supplemented by ultrafast femtosecond laser spectroscopy arrays. Semiclassical barcodes are obtained as digital look-up tables linking physical, discrete prime bits and regional atomic geometry to number-theoretic eigenvalues along the framework. By executing parallel symmetry tests that assemble identical target molecules across entirely different chronological tracks of elemental additions, the protocol verifies that path history is erased by the final minimal-energy spatial geometry. Statistical validation requires the universal barcode library to maintain an absolute structural persistence score of under multi-vector environmental stress profiles across all variable chronological assembly tracks, confirming a completely unique, non-degenerate prime-number sequence footprint. All three of these experiments feature conditional, alternative success criteria, explicit failure thresholds, and automated parametric tuning loops designed to dynamically adjust variables and rerun the assays. Finally, this work discusses future applications and directions of this technology and implications for quantum physics and biology if the three tests are successful.

SARS-CoV-2 in the Post-Pandemic Era: Emerging Variants, Long COVID, Diagnostic Advances, Vaccination Strategies and Therapeutic Perspectives

Ms. Vedika S. Patil — 2026-09-12 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.22712949

The emergence of Severe Acute Respiratory Syndrome Coronavirus 2 (SARS-CoV-2) has resulted in a rapidly evolving global health challenge, with Coronavirus Disease 2019 (COVID-19) transitioning from an acute pandemic to a persistent public health concern. Continuous viral evolution has led to the emergence of multiple variants and sub lineages with altered transmissibility, immune evasion, and clinical characteristics. This review provides an updated overview of the evolving landscape of SARS-CoV-2 infection, encompassing epidemiology, viral pathogenesis, emerging variants, diagnostic advancements, vaccination, therapeutic strategies, and post-acute sequelae of COVID-19, commonly referred to as Long COVID. Particular emphasis is placed on the molecular mechanisms underlying SARS-CoV-2 infection, including spike protein-mediated interaction with angiotensin-converting enzyme 2 (ACE2) and host proteases such as transmembrane serine protease 2 (TMPRSS2), as well as the dysregulated immune responses associated with severe disease and organ injury. The review further discusses major SARS-CoV-2 variants, including Alpha, Beta, Gamma, Delta, and Omicron, along with subsequent Omicron sublineages and their implications for immune escape and disease transmission. Advances in molecular, antigen-based, and serological diagnostic approaches, including multiplex RT-PCR and immunoassay-based methods, are also examined. In addition, the impact of vaccination, booster strategies, breakthrough infections, and global disparities in vaccine access is discussed. Emerging evidence concerning Long COVID, including persistent multisystem manifestations and proposed mechanisms such as immune dysregulation, endothelial dysfunction, microclot formation, and mitochondrial abnormalities, is highlighted. Finally, current therapeutic and preventive approaches are reviewed, emphasizing the need for continued surveillance, improved diagnostic strategies, vaccination, evidence-based treatment, and integrated public health measures. Collectively, this review highlights the continuing evolution of SARS-CoV-2 and the importance of adaptive strategies for reducing the long-term clinical and public health burden of COVID-19.

Inpatient Dermatology and Re-emerging Infectious Diseases: Measles, Varicella, and Beyond

Irene Quan, Zoya Siddiqui, Daniella Jaguan, Cuong Nguyen, Lida Zheng — 2026-09-12 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1007/s13671-026-00499-x

Abstract Purpose of Review Re-emerging infectious diseases are increasingly encountered in the inpatient setting and are important to recognize due to their transmissibility as well as their potential for morbidity and mortality. Inpatient dermatologists are well positioned to recognize cutaneous findings that serve as early diagnostic clues and guide further management. This review provides an up-to-date overview of re-emerging infectious diseases relevant to inpatient dermatology, focusing on vaccine-preventable illnesses, climate change-associated infections, and sexually transmitted infections. Recent Findings Declining vaccination coverage, expanding vector habitats driven by climate change, increased globalization and shifting transmission patterns have contributed to the resurgence of diseases including measles, mpox, dengue, and syphilis. Cutaneous manifestations—ranging from morbilliform eruptions and petechiae to purpura fulminans and pustular dermatoses—often precede or parallel systemic disease and may signal life-threatening complications. Summary As new and re-emerging infectious diseases continue their global spread, inpatient dermatologists play a critical role in identifying characteristic cutaneous findings in medically complex inpatient populations. Timely dermatologic recognition can facilitate earlier diagnosis, prompt isolation, and outbreak containment efforts, representing an important strategy for improving individual outcomes and broader public health surveillance.

(Mutation), जिससे पारंपरिक वैक्सीन बेअसर हो जाती हैं। आविष्कारकर्ता बंटी कुमावत ने वायरस के जेनेटिक कोड का गहन विश्लेषण करके एक ऐसा क्रांतिकारी फॉर्मूला खोजा है, जो वायरस के कभी न बदलने वाले अंदरूनी द्वारा नष्ट होने से बचाता है। इसकी अनूठी बनावट के कारण यह वैक्सीन भीषण गर्मी (40°C तक), ठंड या बारिश में भी खराब नहीं होती। इसे बिना किसी रेफ्रिजरेटर (Cold Chain) के सुरक्षित रखा जा सकता है।2. 5' Cap (सुरक्षा लॉक) और 5' UTR (फ़ास्ट स्टार्टर)5' Cap: यह जेनेटिक चेन का सुरक्षात्मक सिरा है। यह हमारे शरीर के सुरक्षा तंत्र (Immune System) को यह संकेत देता है कि यह कोई बाहरी दुश्मन नहीं, बल्कि शरीर का ही एक हिस्सा है, जिससे शरीर इसे नष्ट नहीं करता।5' UTR: यह कोडिंग शुरू होने से ठीक पहले का रेगुलेटर है। यह मानव कोशिका के कारखानों (Ribosomes) को बिना समय गंवाए मारबर्ग वायरस से लड़ने का काम तुरंत और बहुत तेज़ी से शुरू करने का आदेश देता है।3. मुख्य कोडिंग क्षेत्र (Core Coding Region) - "असली अचूक हथियार"इस फॉर्मूले की सबसे बड़ी विशेषता इसके कोडिंग क्षेत्र में है। आविष्कारकर्ता ने वायरस के उन तीन प्रमुख हिस्सों को चुना है जो कभी नहीं बदलते (Highly Conserved Regions):Leader Sequence (शुरुआती चाबी): यह वायरस का वह नियामक सिरा है जो म्यूटेशन के दौरान भी 0% बदलता है।NP (Nucleoprotein): वायरस के जेनेटिक कोड (RNA) को लपेट कर रखने वाला बेहद मजबूत अंदरूनी कोर सुरक्षा कवच।L Gene (RNA Polymerase - ज़ेरॉक्स मशीन): यह वायरस का मुख्य इंजन है। इसके बिना वायरस शरीर में अपनी प्रतियां (Copies) नहीं बना सकता।MR191 Monoclonal Antibody Code: इसके साथ ही इसमें मारबर्ग वायरस को जकड़कर मारने वाली विशिष्ट एंटीबॉडी बनाने के निर्देश शामिल किए गए हैं।4. 3' UTR (स्थिरता कार्ड) और Poly-A Tail (आत्मघाती पूंछ)3' UTR: इसका कार्य कोशिका के अंदर mRNA को लंबे समय तक मजबूत और स्थिर बनाए रखना है, ताकि शरीर में पर्याप्त मात्रा में एंटीबॉडीज बन सकें।Poly-A Tail (Suicide Timer): जैसे ही शरीर में पर्याप्त मात्रा में एंटीबॉडीज बन जाती हैं और वायरस खत्म हो जाता है, यह पूंछ धीरे-धीरे खुद छोटी होकर समाप्त हो जाती है। इससे वैक्सीन का कोई भी अंश शरीर में स्थायी रूप से जमा नहीं रहता। 🧪 वैज्ञानिक समीकरण (Scientific Equation)इस पूरे फॉर्मूले को निम्नलिखित वैज्ञानिक समीकरण के रूप में देखा जा सकता है:\(\text{Universal\ Vaccine\ (mRNA-LNP)}=\text{Thermostable\ LNP}+[5^{\prime }\text{\ Cap}+5^{\prime }\text{\ UTR}+(\textbf{Leader}+\textbf{NP}+\textbf{L\ Gene}+\text{MR191\ Code})+3^{\prime }\text{\ UTR}+\text{Poly-A\ Tail}]\)🌟 इस आविष्कार के मुख्य लाभ (Key Advantages)म्यूटेशन प्रूफ सुरक्षा (100% Mutation Proof): मारबर्ग वायरस अपना बाहरी रूप कितना भी बदल ले, यह वैक्सीन शरीर के इम्यून सिस्टम को उसके अंदर छिपे Leader Sequence, NP और L Gene को पहचानना सिखा देती है। वायरस इस वार से बच नहीं सकता।ऑल-वेदर स्टेबिलिटी (All-Weather Stability): इस फॉर्मूले को दुनिया के सबसे पिछड़े, ग्रामीण और गर्म इलाकों में भी बिना किसी फ्रीजर या विशेष कोल्ड चेन के आसानी से ले जाया और स्टोर किया जा सकता है।वायरस की रीढ़ यूनिवर्सल mRNA-LNP थेरेपीआविष्कारकर्ता (Inventor): बंटी कुमावत (Banti Kumawat)विषय: वायरोलॉजी, नैनो-मेडिसिन और एंटीवायरल थेरेप्यूटिक्स📝 १. परिचय (Introduction)मारबर्ग वायरस (Marburg virus) एक है। इसका मुख्य कारण यह है कि वायरस की बाहरी सतह (Glycoprotein) लगातार म्यूटेट होकर रूप बदलती रहती है।आविष्कारकर्ता बंटी कुमावत द्वारा परिकल्पित यह फॉर्मूला केवल एक पारंपरिक वैक्सीन नहीं है, बल्कि Awareness, Knowledge, and Acceptance of Human Papillomavirus (HPV) Vaccination Among University Students in Pakistan

Umm-e- Habiba, Rida Zainab — 2026-09-12 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.22725002

This record contains an abstract reporting a cross-sectional survey study assessing awareness, knowledge, and acceptance of Human Papillomavirus (HPV) vaccination among university students in Pakistan. The study examines students’ awareness of HPV infection and its association with cervical cancer, knowledge of HPV vaccination, willingness to receive the vaccine, and perceived barriers to vaccination. Data were collected from approximately 300 undergraduate university students from diverse academic disciplines using a structured questionnaire. The study focuses on identifying gaps in HPV-related knowledge and understanding factors that may influence HPV vaccine acceptance, including accessibility, cost, health education, and cultural or social barriers. The findings are intended to contribute to the understanding of HPV vaccination awareness among university students in Pakistan and highlight the potential importance of university-based health education and awareness initiatives for improving vaccine acceptance and supporting cervical cancer prevention. Authors: Umm-e-Habiba; Rida Zainab

Canine Parvovirus: The Tiny Virus Behind a Life-Threatening Disease in Dogs

Kalyani Putty — 2026-09-12 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.22719311

Introduction Dogs are among the closest companions of humans, but their health can be threatened by several infectious diseases. Among these, canine parvovirus (CPV) is one of the most important and feared viral infections, particularly in puppies. The disease can progress rapidly from apparently mild illness to severe vomiting, haemorrhagic diarrhoea, dehydration, leukopenia, sepsis and death. What makes canine parvovirus particularly challenging is its combination of high contagiousness, environmental persistence, rapid disease progression and continuous viral evolution. Although effective vaccines are available, parvovirus continues to cause outbreaks, especially among unvaccinated or incompletely vaccinated puppies. Recent molecular studies have added another dimension to the problem, demonstrating that CPV is not a genetically static virus and that different variants continue to circulate and evolve in different geographical regions [1–4].

Clinical and economic impact of bivalent respiratory syncytial virus prefusion F (RSVpreF) vaccination in older adults: a cost-effectiveness analysis for Chile

Rafael Bolanos, Tomas Eduardo Abbott, Ali Shajarizadeh, Nishu Gaind, Ramandeep Kaur — 2026-09-12 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.6084/m9.figshare.33653219

Respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) is a major cause of lower respiratory tract disease in older adults in Chile. Chile’s National Immunization Technical Advisory Group (NITAG) recommended RSV vaccination for adults aged ≥60 years with risk factors for severe illness. This study estimated the cost-effectiveness of bivalent RSVpreF vaccination among at-risk older adults in Chile. A population-based Markov model projected RSV-related outcomes under no vaccination and RSVpreF vaccination over the cohort lifetime. Cost-effectiveness was assessed from healthcare and societal perspectives in 2025 Chilean Pesos (CLP) and US Dollars (US$), with costs and outcomes discounted at 3% annually. Sensitivity analyses tested the model robustness. RSVpreF vaccination was projected to prevent 14,956 hospitalizations, 50,419 emergency department visits, and 124,849 physician office visits; avert CLP76 billion (US$82 million) in direct medical costs and CLP305 billion (US$328 million) in indirect costs; and generate 7,079 quality-adjusted life-years (QALYs). At a threshold of CLP15,977,348 (US$17,180) per QALY gained, the maximum cost-effective vaccine price was CLP100,480 (US$108) and CLP265,261 (US$285) from the healthcare and societal perspectives respectively. RSVpreF vaccination would reduce RSV burden and likely be cost-effective for older adults in Chile.

SpoVG as a pleiotropic regulator modulating Listeria monocytogenes biofilm formation and multi-dimensional niche establishment

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Changzheng Shi, Ping Zhu, Ruili Li, Huan Chen, Fanqiang Meng — 2026-09-12 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s41538-026-01134-6

The formation of Listeria monocytogenes biofilms poses significant safety challenges, including antimicrobial resistance and cross-contamination, governed by intricate multi-dimensional regulatory networks. This study focuses on the global regulator SpoVG, elucidating its role in L. monocytogenes biofilm development. Deletion of the spoVG revealed its broad regulatory role on biofilm formation across diverse L. monocytogenes isolates. RNA-seq analysis demonstrated that spoVG modulates biofilm formation by transcriptionally regulating key genes in quorum sensing, chemotaxis, and the phosphotransferase system (PTS). Notably, SpoVG employs a dual regulatory mechanism, acting as a DNA-binding protein and an RNA-binding protein that modulates the stability of selected quorum sensing and motility related genes. Protein interaction assays confirmed SpoVG physically associates with cold-shock proteins (CspB/CspD) and cell wall biosynthesis enzymes (GlmU), suggesting SpoVG coordinates stress adaptation and biofilm matrix biosynthesis. This study establishes a comprehensive SpoVG-mediated regulatory network, providing a theoretical foundation for developing novel anti-biofilm strategies targeting SpoVG to combat L. monocytogenes contamination in food processing environments.

Severe Varicella in Children Under Five Years of Age in the Context of Herd Immunity, Vaccine Hesitancy, and the COVID-19 Pandemic in Brazil, 2008-2023

Maria Isabel do Nascimento, Alcenir Tavares Valente Junior, BEATRIZ DE VASCONCELOS FALCÃO, Marina Pereira da Silva, Mauricio Souza Leão — 2026-09-12 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.18502/jfrh.v20i2.22614

Objective: Varicella (chickenpox) is a highly contagious disease, and its vaccine was incorporated into the Brazilian National Immunization Program in 2013. Objectives: (i) To analyze trends in hospitalization rates for severe chickenpox by age; (ii) To assess the impact of the interventions “anti-vaccine movement/vaccine hesitancy” and “COVID-19 pandemic” on hospitalization rates. Materials and methods: Classical Time Series (CTS) and Interrupted Time Series (ITS) methods were applied to estimate the impact of the anti-vaccine movement/vaccine hesitancy (intervention-1) that occurred in 2016 and the COVID-19 pandemic (intervention-2) that began in 2020 on hospitalization rates among children under 5 years old, stratified by groups based on vaccine-ineligible (< 1 year old) and vaccine-eligible (1-4 years old) children. Results: CTS results supported the potential for herd immunity effects, showing an annual decline of -17% and -14% in hospitalization rates for vaccine-eligible (1-4 years) and vaccine-ineligible (< 1 year) groups, respectively. The ITS model for vaccine-ineligible children showed an upward trend (coefficient 0.35) after intervention-1(isolate) (p=0.001), as well as after intervention-1 (coefficient 0.42; p 0.011) adjusting for intervention-2 (p=0.135). For vaccine-eligible children, the ITS model revealed an upward trend (coefficient 0.14) only after intervention-1 (isolate) (p=0.006). Conclusion: The decline in varicella hospitalizations indicates a herd immunity effect among vaccine-ineligible children. ITS analysis reveals that the anti-vaccine movement/vaccine hesitancy contributed to increased hospitalizations in both vaccine-eligible (1-4 years) and vaccine-ineligible (<1 year) groups, while the COVID-19 pandemic had no discernible impact.

Molecular detection and descriptive epidemiology of fowl adenovirus infection in broiler chickens

Z. A. A. Al-Talabani — 2026-09-12 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.31210/spi2026.29.02.25

Fowl adenoviruses represent substantial viral pathogens distributed across poultry sectors globally, being etiologically linked with severe disease complexes that induce devastating economic losses in broiler production. This study was conducted to establish definitive molecular evidence of fowl adenovirus infection in commercial broiler chickens within the Diyala Governorate using an optimized real-time polymerase chain reaction framework and to mathematically model its regional distribution boundaries. For this purpose, a total number of 96 clinical liver tissue specimens were aseptically collected from 32-day-old broiler chickens displaying clinical indications of hepatic dysfunction across various private operations. Following tissue processing and lysis, viral genomic DNA was isolated using standard silica-membrane extraction matrices. Molecular screening was executed via a qualitative real-time PCR (qRT-PCR) platform utilizing specific oligonucleotide primers (L1-F: 5′-GACGGCATTTGGGGAGGAATT-3′ and L1-R: 5′-GCTGAGGATTGAGCTTCACCC-3′) targeting the hypervariable loop L1 region of the viral hexon gene, followed by a continuous post-amplification dissociation gradient analysis from 58°C to 95°C. Out of the total number of examined specimens, 20 samples returned positive diagnostic outcomes, establishing a regional viral infection prevalence rate of 20.8%. Descriptive statistical variance modeling successfully projected the true expected prevalence limits within a stable 95% confidence interval ranging from 12.7% to 28.9%, maintaining an estimated standard error of 4.14. Furthermore, a non-parametric Chi-square goodness-of-fit test demonstrated an intense, highly significant deviation from theoretical uniform frequencies (χ² = 32.66, df = 1, P < 0.001), determining a powerful localized infectious pressure across the regional poultry ecosystem despite the statistical predominance of negative samples (79.2%). The recorded kinetic amplification plots manifested rapid exponential fluorescence signal emergence between cycles 15 and 22, while melt curve evaluation demonstrated sharp, singular dissociation peaks centered at approximately 85°C, validating absolute amplicon specificity and confirming the total absence of primer-dimer artifacts or non-specific carryover products. These empirical data determine that fowl adenovirus circulation is firmly established at a substantial frequency within the regional poultry networks, confirming that real-time PCR remains an exceptionally rapid, high-fidelity, and dependable diagnostic screening tool for early epidemiological mapping.

Monophosphoryl lipid A, the Toll-like receptor 4 agonist, mitigates acute radiation syndrome via hematopoietic stem cells regeneration

Hyun Jung Ji, Yeongkag Kwon, Joon Hwa Lee, Ki Bum Ahn, Hae-Ran Park — 2026-09-12 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1016/j.biopha.2026.119931

Hematopoietic acute radiation syndrome (H-ARS) is an unmet medical requirement. Current cytokine-based countermeasures, such as granulocyte colony-stimulating factors, provide limited benefits once the surviving hematopoietic stem and progenitor cell pools are severely depleted. Toll-like receptor (TLR) agonists have been proposed as radiomitigators; however, their efficacy across ligands and receptor subtypes has not been systematically compared under equivalent conditions. Here we screened ten TLR agonists spanning eight receptor subtypes in a lethally irradiated mouse model and found that their radiomitigative efficacy was ligand-specific rather than receptor-class-specific. Monophosphoryl lipid A (MPLA), a detoxified TLR4 agonist, and FSL-1 (TLR2/6) exerted the strongest protective effects. MPLA conferred dose-dependent survival benefits when administered after lethal total-body irradiation, and this protection was substantially abolished by myeloid differentiation primary response 88 (MyD88) inhibition and partially reduced by competitive TLR4 blockade, suggesting that the TLR4-MyD88 signaling axis contributes substantially to the radiomitigative effect. Mechanistically, MPLA did not prevent the initial radiation-induced depletion of the bone marrow (BM), but markedly accelerated the subsequent regenerative phase, restoring multilineage peripheral blood counts, splenic and BM colony-forming activity, marrow architecture, and absolute numbers of hematopoietic stem/progenitor cell populations and mesenchymal stem/stromal cells. Regeneration was accompanied by selective induction of erythropoietin and thrombopoietin in the BM without broad changes in myeloid- or lymphoid-directed cytokines. Since MPLA already has an established human safety record as a licensed vaccine adjuvant, these findings support its repurposing as a potential radiomitigator for hematopoietic acute radiation syndrome.

Supplementary material from "Evolving cognitive environment drives critical transitions in voluntary vaccination dynamics"

Hsuan‐Wei Lee, Mao-Chieh Chin — 2026-09-12 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.6084/m9.figshare.c.8710100.v1

Why do voluntary vaccination programs remain unstable despite proven collective benefits? Existing game-theoretic models assume that populations employ uniform decision frameworks with fixed cognitive environments. We develop a co-evolutionary framework coupling seasonal epidemic dynamics with vaccination decisions guided by two competing cognitive systems: socially oriented game cognition versus self-interested vaccine cognition. Their dynamic interplay generates bistable equilibria, hysteresis and critical phase transitions entirely absent from cognitively homogeneous models. Through systematic parameter space exploration across transmission rates, recovery rates, vaccine efficacy and vaccination costs, we establish that game structure proves decisive for population outcomes. Cooperation-favouring dilemmas sustain high coverage below sharp cost thresholds, whereas defection-favouring structures trap populations in persistent low-coverage states. Critical cost thresholds exhibit saturation: beyond moderate vaccine efficacy, further improvements yield diminishing behavioural returns. Cognitive composition evolves as a slow variable amplifying path dependence, wherein initial coverage disproportionately determines long-term equilibria through self-reinforcing cascades. These dynamics expose fundamental nonlinearities in intervention design. Subsidies exhibit threshold effects near critical boundaries; communication strategies must target dominant cognitive frameworks rather than disease severity, and early-stage campaigns possess outsized importance. This coupled dynamics approach extends naturally to other voluntary health behaviours where individual rationality conflicts with collective welfare.

Molecular Detection of Bovine Papillomaviruses in Cattle in Konya Province: The Emergence of BPV-12

— 2026-09-12 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.29261/pakvetj/2026.192

Bovine papillomaviruses cause a contagious viral disease in cattle known as papillomatosis.This study was conducted to determine the molecular presence of papillomavirus in cattle raised in Konya Province.For this purpose, a total of 25 papilloma lesion samples developing in the head, neck, and teat regions of 25 cattle raised for milk and meat production in the Ahirli, Bozkir and Yalihuyuk districts of Konya Province were collected.These lesions were analyzed at the molecular level using the conventional PCR method with the FAP59/64 and MY11/09 consensus primers.While no positivity was detected in any of the samples analyzed using the FAP59/64 primer pair, positive results were obtained in 2 of the 25 samples (8%) examined with the MY11/09 primer pair.In the second-stage analysis performed using type-specific primers in all samples, Bovine Papillomavirus (BPV) types were detected in 24 of the 25 samples (96%).BPV-3, BPV-5 and BPV-11 types were not detected in any of the samples.The BPV types identified using type-specific primers were determined to be BPV-1, BPV2, BPV-4, BPV-6, BPV-8, BPV-9, BPV-10 and BPV-12.In the overall distribution, BPV-12 (95.83%) was identified as the most prevalent type for the first time.Macroscopic examination revealed that 52% of the lesions were classified as having a typical morphological form.No statistically significant association was found between the macroscopic appearance, anatomical localization of the lesions, and the detected BPV types (P>0.05).In conclusion, various BPV types responsible for papillomatosis were determined to be present in cattle raised in Konya Province.

Ethical and social experiences of vaccination, belonging, and exclusion under South Korea’s vaccine passport: a qualitative interview study

S. J. Yun — 2026-09-12 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s12910-026-01599-w

Abstract Background Despite the introduction of vaccine passports with the aim of facilitating pandemic control, their compulsory nature gave rise to controversies, debates, and ethical and social concerns. Existing literature has predominantly focused on vaccine passport acceptance or vaccine hesitancy, with empirical data on vaccine passport usage remaining scarce. This study addresses this gap by examining the perceived influence of vaccine passports on vaccination and the social and ethical issues associated with their implementation in South Korea. Methods This qualitative research was conducted in South Korea in 2022 and 2023. Convenient, purposive and snowball sampling techniques were employed to recruit participants across different vaccination statuses, occupations, and age groups for semi-structured interviews. Of the seventy-six interviews conducted, seventy were purposively selected for thematic analysis, based on detailed discussions regarding the rationale for vaccination and perceptions of the vaccine passport. The data were transcribed verbatim, and the initial coding, categorising, and reviewing of themes were conducted repeatedly. Results The study’s findings indicated that the predominant rationale for interviewees opting for vaccination was to continue to socialise and avoid disadvantages, rather than solely reinforcing the immune system. The mandatory scheme gives rise to socio-ethical concerns, captured in five themes: human rights concerns, stigmatisation, social discrimination and exclusion, easier surveillance, and eroded trust. Most respondents perceived that the government compelled vaccination and exercised control to ensure population conformity to its policies through the introduction of a vaccine pass, audible alerts, restrictions, and social pressure. This study further demonstrated that the vaccine passport functioned as a new digital citizenship, based on users’ vaccine status, rather than merely serving as a certificate. This article suggests that state-mediated exclusion through compliance requirements may produce a category of individuals who find themselves in newly identified vulnerable positions, particularly comprising those who had never previously been categorised as vulnerable groups. Conclusion Even during crises, fostering social discourse and upholding good governance are required to address socio-ethical problems that may arise from countermeasures. Furthermore, policymakers should consider the long-term implications of their decisions and facilitate meaningful public deliberation.

How Alphaviruses Modulate Host Gene Expression: A Systematic-Narrative Synthesis of Host-Virus Response, Key Genes, and Potential Therapeutic Targets

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Amanda Henriques Gonçalves Marinho Gomes, Kamilly Soares de Almeida, Breno Henrique Booz Carvalho Corrêa, José Carlos de Magalhães — 2026-09-12 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.17605/osf.io/xzfht

Alphaviruses are positive-sense single-stranded RNA viruses belonging to the family Togaviridae; they are also part of the non-taxonomic group of arboviruses and constitute a group of arboviruses of major which are of great clinical and epidemiological relevance worldwide. These viruses can be broadly classified into encephalitic and arthritogenic alphaviruses. Important examples include Chikungunya virus (CHIKV), Mayaro virus (MAYV), Venezuelan equine encephalitis virus (VEEV), Ross River virus (RRV), Sindbis virus (SINV), and Semliki Forest virus (SFV), among others. Despite their substantial impact on global public health, characterized by the risk of new outbreaks, high transmission rates, and frequently debilitating or potentially fatal clinical manifestations, effective and specific therapeutic options, including antivirals and vaccines, remain limited for most alphavirus infections. This represents an important public health vulnerability, particularly in tropical and subtropical regions. To address this concerning scenario, a comprehensive understanding of the complex interactions between alphaviruses and their hosts is essential for identifying determinants of viral pathogenicity and, consequently, factors associated with disease severity. In this context, transcriptomic and gene expression profiling approaches have become valuable tools for investigating host responses to viral infection. These approaches provide high-resolution information on changes in host gene expression during Alphavirus infection, contributing to the elucidation of molecular mechanisms underlying disease pathogenesis. They can also provide important insights into the identification of biomarkers and molecular signatures, the upregulation or downregulation of specific genes, and the modulation of inflammatory, oxidative, immune, and other biological pathways. Accordingly, this review provides a comprehensive systematic-narrative synthesis of studies investigating alphavirus–host interactions through gene expression profiling. Studies assessing host gene expression in response to alphavirus infection using approaches such as RNA sequencing (RNA-seq), single-cell RNA sequencing (scRNA-seq), microarrays, RT-qPCR, and other relevant gene expression technologies will be considered across different host species, tissues, experimental models, and infection conditions. Particular emphasis will be placed on studies investigating how alphaviruses modulate key pathogenic pathways, immune responses, inflammation, tissue-specific damage, and other biological processes associated with infection. By integrating evidence from host gene expression profiles, this review aims to highlight the major transcriptional alterations and biological pathways associated with alphavirus pathogenesis. Such integration may provide insights into virus–host interactions and support the identification of candidate molecular targets for therapeutic development, early diagnostic tools, and effective preventive strategies. Therefore, the general objective of this study is to integrate the available evidence on the modulation of host gene expression during alphavirus infection, seeking to elucidate virus–host interactions, molecular mechanisms associated with pathogenesis, and potential key factors involved in these processes. The specific objectives are to: Describe the state of the art regarding alphavirus infections, providing a clinical and epidemiological context for these viruses; Characterize host transcriptional responses to alphavirus infection, identifying similarities and differences in the molecular mechanisms activated or suppressed across different alphaviruses and host systems; Describe molecular mechanisms associated with alphavirus pathogenesis and other biological processes through the integration of differentially expressed genes (DEGs) and enriched biological pathways; Map relevant genes and biological pathways associated with different stages and processes of alphavirus infection, across different experimental systems, including in vitro, in vivo, and publicly available transcriptomic datasets; Identify key genes and molecular factors involved in alphavirus–host interactions and explore their potential as therapeutic targets; Identify knowledge gaps, recurrent patterns, and sources of heterogeneity in the existing literature on alphavirus-associated host gene expression. Through this integrative approach, the review is expected to generate sufficient evidence to support hypotheses and future investigations into therapeutic interventions, including the manipulation, editing, or silencing of specific host genes whose modulation or inhibition may impair viral replication while preserving host-cell or host-organism viability. Keywords: alphaviruses; host-virus response; transcriptomics; molecular mechanisms; differential gene expression; viral pathogenesis

Potency of a commercial inactivated trivalent foot-and-mouth disease vaccine in local goats of the Diyala Governorate, Iraq

A. H. Al-Zuhairi — 2026-09-12 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.31210/spi2026.29.02.10

Foot-and-Mouth Disease remains one of the most economically devastating infections affecting livestock producers globally. This study evaluated the immunogenicity and field efficacy of a commercial inactivated trivalent vaccine containing serotypes O, A, and Asia-1 in the local goat population of the Diyala Governorate, Iraq, focusing on assessing how host sex and pregnancy status modulate the resulting humoral immune response. A total of 178 clinically healthy local breed goats, comprising 100 females and 78 males aged between 7 and 36 months, were immunized with a single 1 mL dose administered subcutaneously. Paired blood samples were collected immediately before vaccination (Day 0) and at Day 28 post-vaccination to capture peak antibody synthesis. Serological analysis was performed using a commercial solid-phase competitive enzyme-linked immunosorbent assay kit, with antibody levels expressed as percentage inhibition, where a threshold of greater than or equal to 50 % was defined as protective immunity. Evaluation of baseline samples revealed a pre-vaccination seropositivity rate of 15.2 % (27/178), confirming that the virus is endemic to the region. Following immunization, the overall protective seropositivity rate increased significantly to 89.3 % (159/178) by Day 28 (p<0.001), and the mean percentage inhibition values more than doubled across all three viral strains. Host sex exerted no statistically significant influence on vaccine responsiveness (p=0.541), yielding comparable seropositivity rates of 87.2 % for male and 90.8 % for female cohorts. Conversely, pregnancy status imposed a substantial physiological limitation on vaccine-induced immunogenicity. Pregnant goats exhibited a significantly lower protective seropositivity rate of 82.1 % compared to 97.3 % in non-pregnant animals (p=0.026). Furthermore, gestation significantly suppressed antibody synthesis levels for serotypes O (p=0.001) and Asia-1 (p=0.002). In conclusion, while the commercial trivalent vaccine is safe and highly potent, pregnancy-induced immunosuppression significantly reduces its protective efficacy. These findings suggest that small ruminants must be integrated into national control programs, and vaccination campaigns should be strategically timed during non-pregnant phases, such as the early dry period or prior to the breeding season, to optimize herd immunity.

Optimizing multi-tier supply chain ordering with LNN+XGBoost: mitigating the bullwhip effect

Chunan Tong — 2026-09-12 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1108/mscra-08-2025-0054

Purpose Supply chain ordering must balance profit, customer service and upstream order variability (the bullwhip effect). This study evaluates whether a two-stage pipeline–a Liquid Neural Network (LNN) encoder with an adaptive-leak recurrent cell followed by an XGBoost readout–offers a useful operational trade-off for multi-tier ordering, rather than assuming hybrid superiority. Design/methodology/approach An LNN encoder represents temporal demand dynamics and XGBoost regresses its frozen latent states; the encoder is explicitly distinguished from canonical Liquid Time-Constant and closed-form continuous-time networks. Under a leakage-free protocol, hyperparameters are selected on a separate development seed and frozen before testing on untouched days from ten unseen demand seeds. A matched linear-readout ablation, paired statistics with multiplicity correction, an observed-order DataCo replay, cost sensitivity analysis and an end-to-end latency benchmark are used. Findings Transformer ($1.738M), XGBoost ($1.737M) and LNN+XGBoost ($1.737M) attain nearly identical mean system profit, with no Holm-adjusted difference between the hybrid and either baseline. Relative to the exact-matched linear readout, LNN+XGBoost gains $14,477 (95% CI $4,811–$24,143; d_z = 1.07), although the family-wise Holm result (p = 0.072) does not indicate a statistically significant difference. Its manufacturer demand-referenced variance ratio is 2.61 versus 2.44 (XGBoost) and 2.48 (Transformer), with a fill rate of 0.996. The Q-network minimizes variance (1.43) but sacrifices profit and service, the DataCo replay favors standalone XGBoost and the hybrid is not latency-optimal. Originality/value The study provides the first leakage-free, ablation-controlled evaluation of an adaptive-leak-LNN–XGBoost ordering pipeline and characterizes a profit–service–bullwhip trade-off with explicit operating conditions rather than claiming universal superiority.

Comparative Analysis of Smart Meter Testing According to International Standards with AI-Based Methods for Anomaly Detection

Saša Aleksandrov — 2026-09-12 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.62762/tepns.2026.407661

Standard type verification of three-phase smart meters is performed under idealized balanced test conditions that do not fully reproduce the current unbalance, single-phase loading and voltage deviations found on real distribution networks, so that a channel-specific calibration fault can pass undetected. This paper presents a methodology combining standardIEC~62052-11 and EN~50470-3 verification with an extended set of near-real-network test conditions (unbalanced current distribution, single-phase loading, and voltage deviation from nominal), together with a statistical/machine-learning screening step applied to aggregated laboratory verification results and used to flag units for targeted extended validation. Three three-phase static meters were analyzed: two units flagged by the screening procedure and one reference unit. Under standard balanced testing all three units remained within their declared accuracy class, but under extended single-phase loading on one specific current channel the two flagged units exhibited systematic registration errors of up to approximately 4%, traced to an incorrectly recorded calibration coefficient on that channel; the reference unit remained within tolerance throughout. The results show that screening of aggregated verification data can identify meters with hidden channel-specific faults that standard balanced verification alone does not reveal, and support extending routine Type C verification with a small set of unbalanced/single-phase test points. The primary contribution of this work is the extended verification methodology and its validation on a real production case; the AI-assisted screening is employed as a triggering mechanism.

This resource enables investigation of tissue-specific and systemic molecular responses to heat stress in dairy cattle.

Identification and preliminary validation of key exosome-related genes in nasopharyngeal carcinoma through integrative bioinformatics and experimental approaches

Liuqian Wang, Yan Zhang, Li Zhao, Di Ji, Guojing Tan — 2026-09-12 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-026-71538-7

Abstract Exosomes have emerged as significant contributors to the progression of nasopharyngeal carcinoma (NPC). However, the characterization of exosome-related gene (ERG) signatures and their immunological relevance in NPC remains limited. This study systematically identified and validated key ERGs in NPC through integrative bioinformatics and machine-learning approaches. Datasets GSE12452 and GSE53819, in addition to known ERGs, were obtained from public databases. Identification of key ERGs employed machine learning, expression verification, and receiver operating characteristic (ROC) curve analysis. Gene set enrichment analysis (GSEA), immune infiltration analysis, in silico compound-target prediction, and molecular docking were also conducted. The expression of key genes underwent experimental validation in clinical NPC tissue samples. A total of 11 candidate genes emerged from machine learning analysis. Of these, LDHA , PIGR , and POSTN demonstrated significant differential expression and passed ROC curve evaluation in both GSE12452 and GSE53819. GSEA revealed involvement of these genes in pathways related to cell cycle checkpoints and DNA replication. Immune infiltration analysis indicated a significantly lower proportion of neutrophils in NPC samples compared to controls, alongside an increased proportion of type 2 helper T cells. Correlation analysis showed that LDHA and POSTN were negatively correlated with neutrophils, while PIGR displayed a positive correlation. Additionally, drug prediction and molecular docking (binding energy: −8.552 kcal/mol) indicated strong binding between LDHA and CHEMBL2058994. Subsequent 100-ns molecular dynamics simulations affirmed the structural and energetic stability of the LDHA –CHEMBL2058994 complex in silico, suggesting its potential as a candidate for further experimental and pharmacological evaluation in NPC. RT-qPCR validation confirmed significant differential expression for LDHA and PIGR , whereas POSTN exhibited a consistent trend without reaching statistical significance in NPC tissue. This study identified three key ERG candidate genes ( LDHA , PIGR , and POSTN ) with potential biomarker value. RT-qPCR validation confirmed significant differential expression of LDHA and PIGR , while POSTN showed a consistent upregulation trend in nasopharyngeal carcinoma tissues but did not reach statistical significance, requiring further evaluation in a larger sample cohort.

Integrated Systems Toxicology Analysis and Molecular Dynamics Study Reveal the Potential Mechanism by which Benzo[a]pyrene Contributes to Depression in Lung Adenocarcinoma via RNASE1 Upregulation

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Jingzhe Gao, Fuxiang Huang, Kunshuang Shen, Shengnan Zhu, Haibin Liu — 2026-09-12 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1021/acsomega.6c04956

Abstract Benzo[a]pyrene (Bap), a prominent carcinogen found in tobacco smoke and air pollution, is closely associated with lung adenocarcinoma (LUAD) and subsequent depression, though the mechanism underlying this comorbidity remains unclear. LUAD patients were categorized based on depression-related gene expression. Prognostic genes identified through machine learning were cross-referenced with predicted Bap targets. Mendelian randomization (MR) evaluated the causal relationship between RNASE1 and depression. Single-cell RNA sequencing (scRNA-seq), molecular docking, and 100 ns molecular dynamics (MD) simulations (using the CHARMM36 force field and TIP3P water model) were conducted, followed by analyses of RMSD, RMSF, Rg, SASA, and Gibbs free energy landscape. A Bap-exposed mouse model was used to validate key gene expression and depression-like behaviors in vivo. Consensus clustering based on 52 depression-related genes stratified the TCGA-LUAD cohort into two gene signature-based molecular subtypes with distinct expression patterns and prognostic outcomes. Cluster 1 represented a favorable-prognosis subtype with better disease-free survival, whereas Cluster 2 represented a poor-prognosis subtype with stronger depression-related transcriptomic features; however, these clusters should not be interpreted as clinically confirmed depression subgroups because TCGA lacks psychiatric assessment data. Among 57 depression-related prognostic genes intersected with 375 Bap targets, five genes overlapped: CLEC7A, NEK2, RNASE1, ITGAL, and CPA3. MR confirmed that elevated lung RNASE1 increased depression risk (IVW: OR = 1.008, 95% CI: 1.002–1.014, P = 0.005), while CLEC7A and other genes showed no causal link. scRNA-seq localized RNASE1 upregulation to tumor epithelial cells, monocytes, and macrophages. Docking and MD analyses supported stable Bap–RNASE1 binding, involving hydrophobic interactions with key residues and a dominant low-energy conformational state. In vivo, Bap-exposed mice showed prolonged immobility time in the tail suspension test, fewer open-arm entries, and reduced open-arm time in the elevated plus-maze test, accompanied by elevated WBC, MPV, PDW, and increased lung RNASE1 protein expression. Bap may contribute to LUAD-related depression through RNASE1 upregulation. This provides molecular-level mechanistic insight into the cancer–depression comorbidity driven by environmental carcinogens.

Ultrasound‐Driven Piezocatalytic Hydrogen Peroxide Generation and Applications

Dong-Gyu Lee, Hyeon-Min Yu, Youngbo Shim, ByeongJae Min, Ye-Rok Choi — 2026-09-12 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1002/aenm.71488

ABSTRACT While hydrogen peroxide (H 2 O 2 ) serves as a pivotal reagent across biomedical and environmental domains, its centralized production conflicts with the demand for on‐demand, spatially confined delivery. Ultrasound‐driven piezocatalysis directly converts mechanical energy into surface chemistry through piezoelectric polarization without electrodes or photons, enabling oxidant generation precisely where and when needed. Despite progress, the field lacks a unified framework connecting its mechanistic foundations, acoustic physics, and application potential. We systematically examine the two dominant mechanistic models; energy band theory and the screening charge effect, and discuss the H 2 O 2 pathway. The physical role of ultrasound is examined beyond its conventional treatment, distinguishing between pressure‐driven deformation and cavitation‐induced impulsive stress as distinct modes of energy transfer. Rather than a compilation of materials, we outline design strategies through which heterojunction engineering, geometric dimensionality, crystal structure modulation, and surface functionalization enhance charge separation and catalytic H 2 O 2 yield. Furthermore, we highlight applications ranging from cancer immunotherapy and implant infection control to environmental remediation. Crucially, its wireless and light‐free operation enables deployment in deep‐tissue and enclosed scenarios inaccessible to photocatalytic or electrochemical systems. Finally, this review establishes a clear framework for the rational design of next‐generation piezocatalysts.

CellMAGE: cell-type deconvolution for multi-parent population analysis of gene expression

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Robyn L. Ball, Alyssa Klein, Ashley A. Auth, Daniel A. Skelly, Hao He — 2026-09-12 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.64898/2026.09.08.750196

Single-cell RNA-sequencing remains prohibitively expensive for multiparental population (MPP) studies. Existing deconvolution methods treat bulk RNA-seq as genetically anonymous mixtures, but in MPPs, the proportional contribution of each parental strain to each progeny's transcriptome is already known. CellMAGE (Cell-type deconvolution for Multi-parent Analysis of Gene Expression) weights parental cell-type profiles by each progeny's known genetic composition, requiring no model training and no minimum sample size. Validated in 16 Diversity Outbred mice across 12 prefrontal cortex cell types and 23,116 genes, predicted and measured gene expression were statistically equivalent (0.05) in all cell types (pooled Spearman = 0.923, 95% CI: 0.910, 0.934). CIBERSORTx required 96 additional samples to resolve at most 12.4% of genes and only 3 cell types; CellMAGE outperformed it even within this restricted comparison (per-cell-type median : 0.908-0.974 vs. 0.353-0.641). CellMAGE is applicable to any MPP with parental single-cell data, including diploid crop MAGIC populations.

Multi-omics profiling identifies neuroinflammation-related genes and exosomal miRNA as robust diagnostic signatures for Parkinson’s disease

Dongdong Wu, Xinxin Ma, Huimin Chen, Huijing Liu, Jing He — 2026-09-12 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-026-71328-1

Neuroinflammation is a pivotal driver that amplifies the pathogenic cascade within the Parkinsonian brain. Nevertheless, the pathogenic drivers connecting neuroinflammation to PD pathogenesis remain unclear. To elucidate their diagnostic and therapeutic implications, this research sought to identify key neuroinflammation-related genes (NIRGs) and exosomal miRNAs in PD. To comprehensively identify neuroinflammation-related genes (NIRGs) in Parkinson’s disease (PD), we conducted an integrated multi-omics analysis. Publicly available transcriptomic data encompassing microarray (GSE75249, GSE22491), high-throughput RNA-seq (GSE269775), and scRNA-seq (GSE223138) profiles were obtained from the GEO repository. We performed differential analysis to screen for significant transcriptional variations, encompassing both mRNA (DEGs) and miRNA (DE-miRNAs). Functional enrichment analyses were conducted, encompassing pathway analysis via the Kyoto Encyclopedia of Genes and Genomes (KEGG), ontological annotation through Gene Ontology (GO), and pre-ranked gene set enrichment analysis (GSEA). Potential protein-level interactions were explored by constructing a protein-protein interaction (PPI) network with the STRING database. Based on the overlap between DEGs and NIRGs, a machine learning framework incorporating ten machine learning algorithms and their 101 combinations was constructed. Subsequently, a quantitative nomogram was constructed for diagnosis in clinical practice. Additionally, the CellChat and Monocle packages were employed to investigate intercellular signaling and cellular differentiation trajectories, respectively. GeneMANIA, Friends analysis, regulatory network, immune infiltration, drug sensitivity, and molecular docking were also investigated. Bulk RNA-seq data were examined, revealing 426 DEGs. Following intersection analysis and the application of a machine learning framework, we generated a diagnostic model utilizing the expression patterns of five signatures (PTGDS, RTN3, MAG, PROK2, and CNTNAP2). The five-gene signature achieved AUC values of 0.797–0.901 in the training cohort and 0.800–1.000 in the validation cohort, with corresponding sensitivity and specificity ranges of 0.500–0.786 and 0.769–1.000, respectively. The robustness of the model was substantiated through cross-validation with internal and external datasets. The scRNA-seq data analysis revealed seven distinct cell clusters, with monocytes being identified as the predominant cell population. Pseudotime trajectory analysis further elucidated the developmental dynamics of the major monocyte lineage. Additionally, cell-cell interactions revealed that the ligand RETN of monocytes is activated. This systems-level study reveals a pivotal role of neuroinflammation in PD, identifies PTGDS, RTN3, MAG, PROK2, and CNTNAP2 as robust diagnostic biomarkers, and highlights candidate drugs and regulatory pathways for therapy. Our results offer novel perspectives on the neuroinflammatory pathways driving PD and establish a foundation for developing biomarker-driven diagnostic and therapeutic strategies.

tTEscanR: A user-friendly integrative R package for quantifying and visualizing translation efficiency from sequencing data in diverse biological systems

Ana Varas Sanchez, Carlos J Gallardo Dodd, Qun Li, William Gao, Markus Ringnér — 2026-09-12 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.64898/2026.09.08.750101

Translation elongation relies on accurate codon-anticodon pairing. Here, we present tTEscanR, an R package designed to investigate this translational interface. By quantifying mRNA codon demand alongside tRNA anticodon availability, tTEscanR provides scalable estimates of translation rates directly from standard transcriptomic and chromatin accessibility count matrices, where genomic features are represented as rows and experimental conditions as columns. tTEscanR seamlessly integrates into existing bulk and single-cell pipelines and provides modular functions for quality control, filtering, normalization, statistical analysis, and visualization. A structured data object centralizes workflow outputs and associated metadata. A built-in multilevel visualization module generates customizable, publication-ready graphical outputs to facilitate data interpretation and reproducibility of the complex translational landscape. We demonstrate the utility of tTEscanR across cancer biology, aging, and neurodegeneration datasets, uncovering critical translational regulatory programs overlooked by conventional analyses. tTEscanR is available in an open-source repository as a standalone tool or workflow plug-in.

Determining the age of single cells using scMLEAge

Chanyue Hu, Matteo Pellegrini — 2026-09-12 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s41514-026-00492-5

Abstract Aging is a complex biological process marked by a gradual decline in physiological function that contributes to increased vulnerability to disease and mortality. Numerous studies have investigated the cellular and molecular aspects of aging at single-cell resolution, yet the heterogeneity of cellular aging in an individual remains poorly understood. To enhance our ability to study aging at the single cell level, we developed a statistical framework to predict the age of individual cells based on their transcriptomic profiles. Our Bayesian approach estimates the most likely age of a cell given its read counts. We applied the model to data from Tabula Muris Senis and examined organ- and cell-type-specific transcriptomic signatures of aging. Compared with standard regression-based methods, our framework achieved higher predictive accuracy. We show that scMLEAge is a powerful tool for dissecting the cellular heterogeneity of aging and age-related functional decline.

High ammonium suppresses nitrate transporter expression and constrains thermal plasticity in the green macroalga Codium cylindricum

Kai‐Le Zhong, Pamela A. Fernández, Bayden D. Russell, Juan Diego Gaitán‐Espitía — 2026-09-12 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.64898/2026.09.10.750591

Nitrogen source and availability can modulate thermal stress responses in marine macroalgae, yet the transcriptional mechanisms underpinning these effects in green seaweeds remain poorly understood. We tested how three N sources (ammonium, nitrate, urea) at low (5 μM) and high (100 μM) concentrations influence the thermal physiology and gene expression of Codium cylindricum. Thermal performance curves were constructed for growth, photosynthesis, and pigment content across a 5–29 °C gradient, coupled with transcriptomic analysis at three key temperatures. At high concentrations, ammonium reduced maximum growth rate by 25% and lowered the thermal optimum relative to nitrate and urea, while nitrate supported the broadest thermal performance. Urea constrained thermal breadth regardless of concentration. These responses were linked to differential expression of Nitrogen assimilation genes. Ammonium suppressed nitrate transporter genes (NRT) and nitrite transporter (FNT), and drove pigment accumulation with rising temperature despite reduced growth, suggesting resource reallocation from growth to photoprotection. In contrast, nitrate upregulated photosystem and phosphorylation-related genes. Our findings demonstrate that Nitrogen source and concentration jointly determine the thermal threshold of C. cylindricum, with high ammonium reducing both maximum growth and the temperature at which it is achieved. This highlights how shifting coastal nutrient regimes may alter macroalgal resilience under ocean warming.

Control efficacy of fermentation extracts from Streptomyces violaceusniger 5–6 against postharvest mango anthracnose and associated host defense responses

Tianyan Yun, Yibo Bai, Xiaoyan Zheng, Tao Jing, Zhiguo Dang — 2026-09-12 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1016/j.postharvbio.2026.114709

Mango anthracnose caused by Colletotrichum gloeosporioides is a major postharvest disease. This study evaluated the control efficacy of fermentation extracts derived from Streptomyces violaceusniger 5–6 and investigated their associated antifungal and host-response mechanisms. At 8 days post-inoculation, the 3 ×EC₅₀ extract treatment reduced the disease index from 93.33% in the blank control to 40.00% and decreased the lesion area percentage by 88.99%, while maintaining postharvest fruit quality. The extracts were also associated with increased activities of defense-related enzymes, including PAL, POD, PPO, CAT, and SOD. Transcriptomic and WGCNA analyses identified responsive genes associated with plant–pathogen interaction, calcium signaling, and the plant MAPK signaling pathway, suggesting that these pathways may participate in extract-associated defense responses. Untargeted metabolomic analysis identified several putatively annotated co-culture-associated metabolites, and a commercially available Ansamitocin P-3 standard exhibited antifungal activity, supporting its consideration as a candidate bioactive metabolite. qRT-PCR analysis of representative DEGs showed expression patterns consistent with the RNA-seq results, supporting the reliability of the transcriptomic analysis. However, the functions of these candidate genes remain to be experimentally validated, and the metabolite identities were not comprehensively confirmed by targeted analytical methods. Further functional and chemical validation is therefore required. These findings indicate that the fermentation extracts have potential to reduce postharvest mango anthracnose through direct antifungal activity and defense-associated host responses.

Integrated transcriptomics and metabolomics reveal key metabolic pathway responses in Myzus persicae (Sulzer) to facilitate arbovirus transmission under cold stress

Mengshuang Yao, Yingqin He, Zhili Li, Lingyi Liu, Changhao Lu — 2026-09-12 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s42003-026-10939-w

Cold stress markedly alters the ability of Myzus persicae to acquire and transmit Potato virus Y (PVY). Using a synchronized multiomics approach combining electropenetrography, untargeted metabolomics and RNA-Seq, we found that cold stress reduced PVY expression and host choice rate and increased transmission efficiency, feeding frequency, and probing activity. At 5°C, aphids oriented toward PVY-infected tobacco within 2 h, with individuals acclimated for 1 day responding earlier than those exposed for 3 days. Metabolomic profiling revealed consistent enrichment of ABC transporters, pathogenic Escherichia coli infection, and glycerophospholipid metabolism across treatments. Transcriptomic analysis revealed significant enrichment of fatty acid and steroid hormone biosynthesis under cold stress. Together, these data indicate that cold stress induces coordinated changes in behavior, metabolism, and transcriptional activity that enhance PVY transmission. The findings reveal interconnected behavioral and molecular adaptations through which low temperature increases vector competence. Cold stress (5°C) reprograms Myzus persicae to enhance potato virus Y transmission: aphids orient earlier to infected plants, increase phloem feeding, and show higher survival and transmission. Multiomics reveal changes in ABC transporters, lipids, and gene expression.
